| OpenCV (`opencv-python`) | Template matching on cropped screenshots — *deferred*; the perceptual-hash signal is done with Pillow + numpy instead, so OpenCV is only taken up if/when template matching is actually built | Apache-2.0 | Defer (not yet a dependency) |

**Tier 4 — human-like interaction**
| Tool | Role | License | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| Playwright mouse/keyboard API | Native scroll, drag, click, hover primitives | Apache-2.0 | Depend (already listed above) |
| `ghost-cursor` (algorithm) | Bezier-curve mouse-path jitter, ported to Python | MIT (original Node lib) | Reference |

**Optional AI fallback (off by default)**
| Tool | Role | License | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| Anthropic/OpenAI API | Vision-based element grounding, last resort only | Commercial API | Optional, pluggable |
| Ollama + a local VLM (e.g. Moondream) | Same, fully local instead of cloud | MIT / model-dependent | Optional, pluggable |

**API surface discovery**
| Tool | Role | License | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| Playwright `record_har_path` | Native HAR capture, no proxy needed | (covered above) | Depend |
| mitmproxy2swagger | Clusters captured traffic into an OpenAPI spec | MIT | Depend |
| ~~mitmproxy~~ | ~~Only if a flow-format conversion step is needed to feed mitmproxy2swagger~~ — **spike resolved: not needed**, mitmproxy2swagger reads Playwright HAR natively (§2b spike note) | MIT | Not needed |
| `gql` (or a raw POST) | GraphQL introspection query | MIT | Optional convenience |

**Signals catalog**
| Tool | Role | License | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chrome DevTools Protocol (via Playwright's CDP session) | Performance metrics, HeapProfiler snapshots, low-level Network events | (browser-native, no separate package) | Depend |

No new libraries needed for storage state, console/error capture, or the accessibility tree — all native Playwright APIs already counted above.

**Operational essentials**
| Tool | Role | License | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pydantic` | Config field validation, extracted-data schema validation | MIT | Depend |
| `tenacity` | Retry/backoff policy for transient failures | Apache-2.0 | Substituted — hand-rolled in `spoor/operational/retry.py` (Phase 3.5). The policy is a small bounded loop that classifies HTTP outcomes and *returns* a `FetchFailure` (rather than raising after exhaust) and threads the same injectable `sleep` seam as the politeness gate — a better fit than tenacity's decorator/retry-state model at this size. See the Phase-3.5 decision note in §2d. |
| stdlib `json`/`csv`/`sqlite3` | Default output sinks | PSF | Depend |
| `pyarrow` | Optional Parquet output | Apache-2.0 | Optional |

**Exploration mode + wiki (v2, §2e)**
| Tool | Role | License | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| Crawljax (`zaproxy/crawljax`, under OWASP ZAP) | State-abstraction-function algorithm for dedup/exploration | Apache-2.0 | Reference |
| MkDocs (or Docusaurus) | Static site generator for the browsable wiki output | BSD-2 (MkDocs) / MIT (Docusaurus) | Depend |
| Mermaid / `pyvis` / `vis.js` | Graph visualization for the state/transition overview | MIT-family | Depend |

**Serving the map (§2f)**
| Tool | Role | License | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mcp` (official Python SDK, `modelcontextprotocol/python-sdk`) | MCP server mode — exposes the map as tools an agent can call | MIT | Depend |
| FastAPI | Plain REST API mode for non-MCP consumers | MIT | Depend |

**Project tooling (not part of the shipped library, but part of building it)**
| Tool | Role | License | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `uv` (or Hatch) | Fast dependency management / packaging, replaces pip+venv juggling | MIT (uv) | Dev tooling |
| `ruff` | Linting + formatting in one tool | MIT | Dev tooling |
| `pytest` | Test runner for everything in §5 | MIT | Dev tooling |
| `hypothesis` | Property-based/mutation testing for tier 3 (§5.3) — the highest-priority test suite in the plan | MPL-2.0 | Dev tooling |
| `toxiproxy` | Network fault injection (latency, drops) for §5.2 chaos testing | Apache-2.0 | Dev tooling |
| `locust` | Load/concurrency testing for the §2d politeness policy | MIT | Dev tooling |
| `pytest-cov` | Coverage tracking | MIT | Dev tooling |
| `mypy` or `pyright` | Optional static type checking, pairs well with `pydantic` models | MIT | Optional dev tooling |
| `typer` (built on `click`) | CLI (`spoor run config.yaml`) | MIT | Depend |
| Docker / `docker-compose` | The §5 test bench (all four archetype fixtures) | Apache-2.0 | Dev/test tooling |
| GitHub Actions | CI (lint, test, the nightly drift job) | N/A (hosted) | Dev tooling |

One thing worth noticing across this whole table: apart from the two API-vendor options in the optional AI fallback row, **every single entry is open source and self-hostable**, and the large majority are permissively licensed (MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD) — consistent with §1's positioning and §3's license choice. Nothing here should force MIT-incompatible obligations onto the finished project.

## 8. Immediate next steps

1. ~~Pick a project name~~ — **Spoor**, confirmed. ~~Pick a PyPI distribution name~~ — **`ht-spoor`**, confirmed (plain `spoor` is taken). Confirm MIT license and register the PyPI name (`ht-spoor`) and the GitHub repo (as `<org-or-username>/spoor` — the bare `github.com/spoor` username is taken by an unrelated account; see §9). Two dormant, different-domain namesakes exist (`microsoft/spoor`, a SourceForge wiki engine) — low collision risk, no trademark concern (§9).
2. Scaffold the repo (Phase 0) and get Crawlee's adaptive crawler running against one real site end-to-end, driven by a single §2a config file and writing JSON output (Phase 1) — this is the fastest path to something demoable.
3. Decide who else, if anyone, is working on this with you — affects how much process (CI, review) is worth setting up now vs. later.
4. ~~Run the mitmproxy2swagger/HAR spike (§2b, item 4) before scoping Phase 2.5 for real~~ — **done**: HAR feeds into mitmproxy2swagger natively, no flow conversion needed (see the §2b spike decision note and the resolved §9 entry). The residual Phase-2.5 choice is library-vs-reimplement, not a technical unknown.

## 9. Open questions & backlog

Tracked here so they don't get lost, sorted by whether they block anything:

**Needs answering before the relevant phase starts:**
- ~~**mitmproxy2swagger + HAR compatibility** (§2b) — confirm via a short spike before Phase 2.5 is scoped for real.~~ — **resolved by spike** (see the "mitmproxy2swagger accepts a Playwright HAR directly" decision note in §2b). mitmproxy2swagger 0.15.0 reads a genuine Playwright HAR natively (`-f har`) and clusters it into an OpenAPI spec; no mitmproxy flow-format conversion needed. Remaining Phase-2.5 scoping choice (not a blocker): depend on mitmproxy2swagger as a library vs. reimplement the bounded clustering directly against HAR to shed its heavy transitive dependencies — the §9 fallback, now a live design option. Drive it as a library, not the CLI (Windows cp1252 progress-bar crash + interactive two-pass workflow).
- ~~**Concrete secret-redaction pattern list** (§2h)~~ — **delivered** as `spoor/security/redaction.py` (see the secret-redaction-pipeline decision note in §2h): a maintained `SECRET_PATTERNS` list behind a default-on `redact(text)` primitive, built before its first shared-output consumer. Ongoing work is extending the list (with a `redaction.feature` example per shape added), not building it.
- ~~**Localization of the §2e destructive-action keyword guard**~~ — **delivered, closes #101** — see the locale-aware-keyword-guard decision note below. The destructive-action classifier itself is no longer English-only.
- **Localization of the input-data-mapping name/label matching (§2g/#103)** — *noted, needs sparring; not yet designed.* The same English-only keyword-matching concern this item originally raised, for a **different** consumer: whatever name/label matching the form-fill data-provisioning mechanism (#103) ends up relying on to recognize a field as e.g. "email" or "password" from its visible label. Not yet designed because #103 itself isn't yet designed. Only bites once §2e leaves read-only mode, so it blocks Phase 5, nothing sooner.

**Deliberately deferred, not blocking anything now:**
- ~~**Sandbox-registry hardening** (§2e)~~ — **delivered**, closes #102 — see the sandbox-registry-backstop decision note above: a declared sandbox now also requires the host to be loopback or private-range.
- **Freshness/staleness policy for §2f (#107), low priority.** Placeholder for v1 is "always show the answer's age, never auto-recheck." The real policy (when Spoor should proactively re-verify vs. wait to be asked) gets decided once there's real usage to learn from — deprioritized explicitly for that reason (see the #107 comment thread): picking a threshold now would be guessing, not deciding, and the current placeholder is a legible, correct default, not a gap. *Mechanism now designed (not yet built), tracked separately:* re-verifying an explored map's transitions is the "freshness by re-observation" design note in §2b/§2f (#211, corrected from an earlier mis-citation as #107) — that settles *how* a re-check would record what changed; *when* Spoor should re-check on its own is this issue's own, narrower open policy question.
- ~~**Generated regression tests only assert additive signals, never flag new/unexpected ones (§2g)**~~ — **partially shipped: `--assert-no-new-signals`, closed #128's own ask.** See the §2g reverse-assertion decision note (closes #128): `spoor explore --gen-tests --assert-no-new-signals` now fails a generated test if the live replay produces a console/storage/network value never recorded anywhere on the transition's replayed path, using only data a normal explore run already has. What remains open is the *bigger* capability this backlog item's design pass pointed at — the "freshness by re-observation" design note (#211, corrected from an earlier mis-citation as #107 — see #107's own comment thread; #107 itself is the unrelated, narrower served-data auto-recheck-policy question — replay + double-replay noise filtering + normalization + persistent baseline + wiki/served surfacing) — which the shipped flag deliberately does not need and does not build: it has no noise normalization, so a value that varies run to run (a timestamp, a random id) can make it noisier than the default suite. #211 remains tracked separately, open, low priority, not urgent, not blocking anything today.
- **Wiki network grouping should use the browser-reported resource type, not a URL-extension guess (§2e, slice 6d follow-on)** — the state page's Documents / Scripts / Styles / Images / Fonts / Media / Data / Other grouping comes from `spoor/exploration/wiki.py:_request_category`, a URL-only heuristic (last-segment extension, `/api/` → Data, extensionless → Document) that its own docstring calls best-effort, because the live driver records only `request.url` (`PlaywrightDriver._on_request`) and `StateSignals.network_requests` is a tuple of URL strings (the 6d decision note above). It mislabels routinely: extensionless or query-served assets (a CDN image at `/img/123`, `/asset?id=…`) and JSON endpoints not under `/api/` all read as Documents, and any extension outside the fixed table falls to Other. Playwright already exposes `request.resource_type` (document, stylesheet, image, media, font, script, texttrack, xhr, fetch, eventsource, websocket, manifest, other), which Chromium derives from how the page *initiated* the request rather than from the URL — so it is no less generic (§0) and needs no response capture. Sketch, not a decision: record the type alongside each URL in the live driver; carry it through `StateSignals` / `TransitionSignals` backward-compatibly (e.g. an optional parallel field defaulting to empty, so fake drivers and existing tests that supply plain URLs still work) with an explicit, documented fallback for an untyped request (fold into Other, or a distinct "Unclassified") rather than silently keeping the extension guess; map the types onto the existing taxonomy (xhr/fetch/eventsource/websocket → Data, stylesheet → Styles, texttrack → Media, manifest/other → Other); and retire `_EXTENSION_CATEGORY`. §2h unchanged — the URL is still redacted before it reaches the wiki; the type is a fixed browser vocabulary, not captured content. Would need BDD first (extend `features/exploration_wiki.feature` and the live `features/exploration_signals_live.feature`, run `pytest -m browser`) plus reconciling `features/README.md`, `docs/OVERVIEW.md` and the 6d note. Presentation accuracy only — doesn't block anything today.
- ~~**Whether a thinner §2g (test-automation generation) should ship before Phase 5**~~ — **decided: yes, shipping incrementally now.** Sub-slice 2g-i (the pure pytest generator from the §2e exploration graph) has shipped against the full exploration graph (see the 2g-i decision note in §2g); the on-disk writer + live-run proof (2g-ii) is the remaining follow-on. The richer configured-§2a-run and human-readable-test-plan shapes remain future work.
- ~~**Input-data provisioning for interactive modes, v1** (§2e/§2g)~~ — **delivered, closes #103** — see the generator-seam decision note below. The two complementary paths the sketch named (Spoor scaffolds a starter config; the user pins specifics) are both real now, not just sketched. **Deliberately not delivered, left as explicit follow-on work, not silently dropped:** the **Mimesis** backend (Faker alone covers v1), **exrex**/`pattern=`-constrained generation, and capturing the richer field semantics the original sketch named (`pattern`/`min`/`max`/`maxlength`, `<select>` options, `required`, name/label/ARIA keywords) — v1's generator is keyed only by the DOM `input_type` signal discovery already captured for #131, nothing new added to `ActionableElement`/discovery. Also not delivered: a standalone, reusable **user-supplied data config** (e.g. "happy-day customer IDs" pinned once and reused across runs/targets) — v1's pinning stays exactly where it already lived, per-field inside one run's `interactive.yaml` scaffold, not a new cross-run config surface. Each of these is a real, separate, not-yet-designed extension, tracked as its own future item rather than reopening #103 for them.

- **Visual capture: screen/state identity + screenshots as surfaced evidence** (§2c/§2e/§2g/§2h) — *in progress (§2e slice 8): the screenshots-in-the-wiki strand of this item is now designed and being built as the staged, opt-in slice 8 (see the slice-8 decomposition note above); 8a shipped the pure-renderer embedding and 8b shipped the live capture, the `render_wiki` image writer, and the `spoor explore --screenshots` opt-in flag, so stage 1 (a full-page screenshot per state, embedded on opt-in) is complete; 8c grouped the images into a `screenshots/` subfolder, 8d added stage-3 per-element clips filling 6e's Screen-capture column, and 8e added the opened-contents capture (open a gate-permitted disclosure element, screenshot what it reveals, Escape-restore) in a new "Opened contents" column — completing the maintainer's "the dropdown and its contents in a separate column" request. The screen/state-identity strand — **(b) below — is now delivered, closes #104; see the visual-state-identity decision note below.*** Two related needs surfaced that the plan doesn't yet cover: (1) telling **visually-distinct screens apart** even when their text/DOM is equivalent — e.g. a "Main screen" that is the *maintenance* home vs. the *order* home — which today's state identity cannot do, because state identity is defined as normalize-the-DOM-and-hash (§2e prior-art note) with **no visual component**, so two such screens collapse to one state — **delivered, see below**; and (2) **seeing the acting element** ("show me the button that gets pressed") and the resulting screen as visual evidence in the §2e wiki and the §2g test record — today the action/transition record carries a page-level state screenshot at most, never the element's own cropped image — **delivered by slice 8d, closes #105** (the Actions table's Screen-capture column embeds a real per-element clip, not a placeholder — see the 8d decision note above). Direction to spar, staged so early value doesn't wait on the v2 wiki (§2e, Phase 5): **(a)** make a screenshot a *kept* local-only artifact (like the HAR/storage captures, §2h) and compute its **perceptual hash** (the `spoor/core/visual.py` dHash already exists, used element-level for tier-3 healing) — the hash is a 64-bit integer, so *surfacing it* on the run summary/map is §2h-clean and already answers "maintenance vs. order screen" without any pixels leaving local — **delivered**; **(b)** fold that visual hash into how screens/states are told apart — **delivered, closes #104** — and attach the element screenshot as a **reference** (local path + hash + dimensions, all text) to the shipped §2b action↔request correlation record — **explored while closing #105, found to be a materially larger change than sketched and explicitly deferred, not silently dropped**: `spoor/api_discovery/correlation.py`'s correlation mechanism only runs during `spoor run`/extraction (`extract.run_report`), keyed by a free-text `Checkpoint.label` at action-sequence granularity, while an element screenshot only exists during `spoor explore`, keyed by (state id, discovery-position) against the exploration graph's `ActionableElement` identity — two structurally separate runners today, with no shared key to join a correlation record to an element screenshot. Attaching one to the other isn't a reference field away; it needs either `explore()` growing its own checkpoint/correlation tracking or `correlate()` learning exploration's element identity — a real architectural connection between two currently-independent subsystems, not scoped here, revisit if a concrete use case calls for it; **(c)** deliver actual **pixels** only as a **curated, explicit export** — the wiki generates locally with images referenced by relative path, and a selected image pack (e.g. a zip alongside the static site) leaves the machine only on a deliberate user action — **resolved, closes #106; see the screenshot-redaction decision note below**. Hard constraints so the eventual design doesn't drift: hashing/screenshotting is generic and stays in `core/`/`visual.py` (§0, already true); binary **cannot** be pattern-redacted, so §2h's text redaction does not make a screenshot safe — pixels reach a shared surface only via the **explicit-opt-in export** §2h already sanctions for the raw cache, with human curation standing in for redaction; and the visual hash from (a) is what makes that curation tractable (dedup near-identical screens, export only the distinct ones).

**Needs a decision eventually, but not urgent / not blocking Phase 0:**
- **CPU/memory/disk cost estimates** — none exist yet for any resolution tier or for exploration-mode runs. Real numbers depend on the §5.2 `locust` load-testing work; treat as a measured *output* of Phase 3.5/Phase 5, not something to guess at now.
- **Maintainer/bus-factor policy** — §6 names "maintenance load" as a risk but doesn't answer who actually handles it when a core dependency (Playwright, Crawlee, mitmproxy2swagger) has a breaking change, or what support-bandwidth promise (if any) goes in the README. Decide before or shortly after public release, not before Phase 0.
- ~~**A GUI front-end (#112)**~~ — **decided and delivered, see §2i: all four slices (gui-1 to gui-4).** Promoted by maintainer direction: a loopback-only local web GUI (`spoor gui`) that is a separate control plane from the read-only §2f serving layer and drives Spoor by running the real CLI as a subprocess. It covers map browsing, explore/run/apply-scaffold jobs, sessions and config editing, and server controls, built as staged slices gui-1…gui-4.
- ~~**A durable queryable store (SQLite) for configs/sessions/state, rendered per-call for MCP**~~ (§2a/§2e/§2f/§2h, #184) — **sparred into a real design, split into epic #216.** The open questions this entry listed are answered: SQLite replaces (not sits alongside) the flat-file convention; one database, not several; sessions become a first-class, named, multi-per-site concept; the store is read-write, written only from `spoor run`/`spoor explore`/a session-management surface, never from the MCP/API serving layer itself (§2f's non-negotiable unaffected). Split into #214 (Phase A: swap `MapStore`'s backing storage) and #215 (Phase B: named/multiple stored sessions, the `spoor session` command) — **both shipped**, see their decision notes under §2f and §2h respectively; epic #216 stays open only as the tracking issue until both are closed out. The per-call bounded-Markdown-rendering idea for MCP remains exactly that, an idea, for whichever future slice needs it — nothing renders that way yet.
- **Decision — do not adopt Crawlee; build the remaining crawl-engine pieces in-house (docs/COMPETITIVE_PLAN.md §3, D1), closing issue #146.** The hands-on spike (posted on #146) found Crawlee's base package genuinely lean (26 packages, no browser/ML/SQL extras pulled in by default) and confirmed `RequestQueue`/`SitemapRequestLoader`/`AutoscaledPool` are usable standalone, outside its `Crawler` orchestration classes — the adoption case looked real. It didn't hold up against what actually got built next: **#169 (link following with scope rules)** and **#170 (sitemap/robots.txt seeding)** — the two biggest items `docs/COMPETITIVE_PLAN.md` §4 marked *Depend* or *Depend-or-Adapt* — both shipped with **zero** Crawlee dependency, at a cost no higher than adopting would have been (#170's sitemap support, in particular, needed no new library at all: `urllib.robotparser.RobotFileParser.site_maps()` already reads the `Sitemap:` directive for free). That leaves only two items where Crawlee would still help: autoscaling concurrency (`AutoscaledPool`) and resumable persisted crawl state (`RequestQueue`'s own disk persistence) — and the second was never simply free from adopting Crawlee either, since reconciling its storage model with Spoor's existing local-cache conventions (§2h) and the already-shipped exploration resume story (§2e) is real design work regardless of which way this decision went. Against that narrowed remainder, the deciding cost is architectural: **Crawlee is asyncio-native throughout; Spoor is synchronous throughout** (`httpx.Client`; `PlaywrightDriver` deliberately on Playwright's *sync* API, not async). Adopting even just `AutoscaledPool` means bridging sync and async somewhere in a codebase that currently has none of that seam — an ongoing structural cost for one feature, not a one-time integration task (the project's own debugging this cycle, chasing a Playwright sync/async bridging issue down to its root cause, is a live demonstration of how subtle that category of bug gets). Concurrency's actual bottleneck is politeness-gated HTTP fetches, not CPU-bound work, so a plain stdlib `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` with per-domain semaphores gets bounded parallel fetching without ever leaving Spoor's existing synchronous world — less adaptive than `AutoscaledPool`'s CPU/memory-aware scaling, but matched to the actual bottleneck and free of the sync/async seam. **Resolution**: §2d's "Crawlee's autoscaling pool already handles per-domain concurrency" claim (corrected above) was the real ROADMAP/code mismatch the competitive plan flagged, and is now fixed; the former crawl-engine mega-issue (#148) is closed, replaced by two scoped, in-house, sync-native issues — concurrency (a bounded worker pool) and resumable persisted crawl state (its own reconciliation-with-existing-resume-story design pass) — each to go through the normal BDD-first workflow when picked up, same as #169/#170 did.
- **Decision — shape of the future optional LLM tier: "LLM once, replay forever" (docs/COMPETITIVE_PLAN.md §3, D2).** Not building the tier itself yet — this only settles the model *if/when* it is: an LLM works out an action or a config once, Spoor records what it decided as a deterministic step, and every later run replays that recorded step without the model — cf. Stagehand's action caching, Crawl4AI's one-time schema generation. This is the fit with Spoor's core determinism guarantee (§1): the model is consulted only for discovery, never something a later run's correctness depends on. Applies uniformly to the tier's three sketched facets (issue #156) — sandboxed LLM form filling, accessibility-tree-driven action discovery, and LLM-generated extraction configs — whichever gets built first. Still fully gated by §1 (never the default run loop, no autonomous goal-driven agents) and by the §2e sandbox-only rule wherever the tier would interact with a target; this decision is about *replay shape*, not about relaxing either.

**Small, easy to close whenever:**
- ~~Formally confirm MIT~~ — **done**, see §3.
- ~~Confirm the actual GitHub org/repo path is free~~ — **checked.** The bare top-level username `github.com/spoor` is taken (an individual account, no public repos, unrelated) — not a blocker: host as `<org-or-username>/spoor` instead, the same way every other project sharing this name does. PyPI's `ht-spoor` remains the distribution name (§3, §8.1).
- ~~Check for name collisions / prior "Spoor" projects~~ — **checked, low risk.** Two pre-existing "Spoor" projects turned up: `microsoft/spoor` (an iOS performance-tracing tool, archived June 2026) and a SourceForge Django wiki engine (dormant since 2013). Both are a different domain from a web-crawler/extraction tool and both are inactive, so collision risk is low; no trademark concern was found. Re-check before any public announcement, but nothing here blocks development or the chosen name.
